=&(x^{0})^{1-\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}}\exp\left(\sum_{g=0}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\left(\frac{\lambda}{x^{0}}\right)^{2g-2}C^{(g)}_{a_{1}\cdots a_{n}}(t,\bar{t})\,\frac{x^{a_{1}}}{x^{0}}\cdots\frac{x^{a_{n}}}{x^{0}}\right).\end{split}

(4.12)

and take the ‘holomorphic limit’
t^{\bar{a}}\to-\mathrm{i}\infty
keeping
z^{a}
and other variables fixed. From this limit one arrives at the wave function in holomorphic polarization
[
93
, (36)]
19
19

19

We adjust the normalization by
x^{\Lambda}
-independent factor such that
\Psi_{\rm hol}
satisfies the holomorphic limit of the holomorphic anomaly equations.

\Psi_{\rm hol}(t,\lambda;x^{\Lambda})=\lambda^{\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}-1}e^{\mathcal{F}^{(1)}(t,-\mathrm{i}\infty)}\widetilde{\Psi}(t,-\mathrm{i}\infty,\lambda;x^{\Lambda})\,.

(4.13)

Using the fact that
C_{a_{1}\dots a_{n}}^{(g)}
are covariant derivatives of
\mathcal{F}^{(g)}(t,\bar{t})
, and that
\partial_{a}\mathcal{K}
vanishes in this limit, one arrives at

\Psi_{\rm hol}(t,\lambda;x^{\Lambda})=(x^{0})^{1-\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}}\exp\left(F(t^{a}+\tfrac{x^{a}}{x^{0}},\tfrac{\lambda}{x^{0}})-\tfrac{(x^{0})^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\left(\mathcal{F}^{(0)}+\tfrac{x^{a}}{x^{0}}\partial_{t^{a}}\mathcal{F}^{(0)}+\tfrac{x^{a}x^{b}}{2(x^{0})^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{ab}^{(0)}\right)\right)

(4.14)

where
F(t^{a},\lambda)=\sum_{g\geq 0}\lambda^{2g-2}\mathcal{F}^{(g)}(t^{a},-\mathrm{i}\infty)
, and the genus 0 subtractions originate from the fact that
C^{(g)}_{a_{1}\dots a_{n}}
only includes correlators with

2g-2+n>0
.

Now, let us consider the effect of the ‘holomorphic limit’
z^{\bar{a}}\to-\mathrm{i}\infty
on the Hodge decomposition (
4.3
).
Using

\left(\int_{X}g^{\Lambda}\wedge\Omega_{3,0};\int_{X}\tilde{g}_{\Lambda}\wedge\Omega_{3,0}\right)=(X^{\Lambda};F^{(0)}_{\Lambda})=X^{0}(1,t^{a};2\mathcal{F}^{(0)}-t^{a}\mathcal{F}_{a}^{(0)},\mathcal{F}_{a}^{(0)})\,,

(4.15)

where
\mathcal{F}^{(0)}_{a}=\partial_{t^{a}}F^{(0)},\mathcal{F}^{(0)}_{ab}=\partial^{2}_{t^{a}t^{b}}\mathcal{F}^{(0)}
,
one finds that in this limit, the real Darboux coordinates
are related to the coordinates in the Hodge decomposition by

\begin{split}\zeta^{0}/X^{0}=&x^{0}\,,\\
\zeta^{a}/X^{0}=&x^{a}+t^{a}x^{0}\,,\\
\tilde{\zeta}_{a}/X^{0}=&\tilde{x}_{a}+\mathcal{F}_{ab}^{(0)}x^{b}+\mathcal{F}_{a}^{(0)}x^{0}\,,\\
\tilde{\zeta}_{0}/X^{0}=&\tilde{x}_{0}-t^{a}\tilde{x}_{a}+(\mathcal{F}_{a}^{(0)}-t^{b}\mathcal{F}_{ab}^{(0)})x^{a}+(2\mathcal{F}^{(0)}-t^{a}\mathcal{F}_{a}^{(0)})\zeta^{0}\,.\end{split}

(4.16)

This implies that
the wave function in the real polarization is related to the wave function in the holomorphic polarization by

\begin{split}&\Psi_{\rm hol}(t,\lambda;x^{\Lambda})\\
=&e^{-\frac{(X^{0})^{2}}{\hbar}\left(\mathcal{F}^{(0)}(x^{0})^{2}+\mathcal{F}_{a}^{(0)}x^{0}x^{a}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{F}_{ab}^{(0)}x^{a}x^{b}\right)}\,\Psi_{\mathds{R}}\left(\zeta^{0}=X^{0}x^{0},\zeta^{a}=X^{0}(x^{a}+t^{a}x^{0})\right)\,.\end{split}

(4.17)

Comparing with (
4.14
) and setting
\lambda=\sqrt{\hbar}/X^{0}
, we see that the genus 0 subtractions cancel and one finds

\begin{split}\Psi_{\mathds{R}}(\zeta^{\Lambda})=&(\lambda x^{0})^{\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}-1}\exp\left(F(t^{a}+\frac{x^{a}}{x^{0}},\frac{\lambda}{x^{0}}\right)\\
=&(\lambda\zeta^{0}/X^{0})^{\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}-1}\exp\left(F(\tfrac{\zeta^{a}}{\zeta^{0}},\tfrac{\lambda X^{0}}{\zeta^{0}}\right)\\
=&Z_{\rm top}(\tfrac{\zeta^{a}}{\zeta^{0}},\tfrac{\sqrt{\hbar}}{\zeta^{0}})\,,\end{split}

(4.18)

where
Z_{\rm top}(t^{a},\lambda)
was defined in (
2.7
).
Thus, we conclude that the topological string partition function
Z_{\rm top}
is equal to the real polarized wave function, and should transform under monodromies according to the metaplectic representation (
4.10
), with
\hbar
replaced by
(\lambda X^{0})^{2}
. Further renaming
\zeta^{\Lambda}\mapsto X^{\Lambda}
and enforcing the Kähler gauge choice
\lambda=1/X^{0}
, we arrive at the transformation property under the
monodromy (
2.2
),

Z^{\prime}_{\rm top}(X^{\prime\Lambda})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\,{\rm det}\,C}}\int e^{-S(X,X^{\prime})}Z_{\rm top}(X^{\Lambda})\,\prod_{\Lambda}\mathrm{d}X^{\Lambda}

(4.19)

for
Z_{\rm top}(X^{\Lambda}):=Z_{\rm top}(X^{a}/X^{0},1/X^{0})=(X^{0})^{1-\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}}\exp(\sum_{g\geq 0}F^{(g)}(X^{\Lambda}))
.

We emphasize that
Z_{\rm top}(X^{\Lambda})
is only defined as an asymptotic series in powers of
1/X^{0}
, and correspondingly the integral in (
4.19
) should be evaluated using the saddle point method, by the usual Feynman expansion around extrema of
F(X^{\Lambda})-S(X,X^{\prime})
with respect to
X^{\Lambda}
.
As an example, restricting to a one-parameter model with
T=X^{1}/X^{0},\lambda=\lambda^{\prime}
for simplicity,
20
20

20

See
[
44
, (2.11)]
for an example of a Feynman expansion up to second order in multi-parameter models, along with a pictorial representation.

\int\mathrm{d}T\exp\left(-\frac{\mathcal{S}(T,T^{\prime})}{\lambda^{2}}+\sum_{g\geq 0}\mathcal{F}^{(g)}(T)\lambda^{2g-2}\right)=\exp\left(\sum_{g\geq 0}\mathcal{F}^{\prime(g)}(T^{\prime})\lambda^{2g-2}\right)

(4.20)

with
S(X^{\Lambda},X^{\prime\Lambda})=(X^{0})^{2}\mathcal{S}(T,T^{\prime})
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{G}(T,T^{\prime})\rangle_{T}\,,\quad\mathcal{G}(T,T^{\prime}):=\mathcal{F}^{(0)}(T)-\mathcal{S}(T,T^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime(1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{2}\log(\partial^{2}\mathcal{G})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime(2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{(2)}-\tfrac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}+(\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)})^{2}}{2\partial^{2}\mathcal{G}}+\tfrac{\partial^{4}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}+4(\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)})(\partial^{3}\mathcal{G})}{8(\partial^{2}\mathcal{G})^{2}}-\tfrac{5(\partial^{3}\mathcal{G})^{2}}{24(\partial^{2}\mathcal{G})^{3}}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime(3)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{(3)}-\tfrac{2{\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}{\partial\mathcal{F}^{(2)}}+{\partial^{2}\mathcal{F}^{(2)}}}{2{\partial^{2}\mathcal{G}}}+\tfrac{4{\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}}{\partial\mathcal{F}^{(2)}}+{\partial^{4}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}+2({\partial^{2}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}})^{2}+4{\partial^{3}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}{\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}+4({\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}})^{2}{\partial^{2}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}}{8({\partial^{2}\mathcal{G}})^{2}}

(4.21)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{{\partial^{6}\mathcal{G}}+12{\partial^{4}\mathcal{G}}{\partial^{2}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}+12{\partial^{4}\mathcal{G}}({\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}})^{2}+20{\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}}{\partial^{3}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}+8{\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}}({\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}})^{3}}{48({\partial^{2}\mathcal{G}})^{3}}-\tfrac{{\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}\left({\partial^{5}\mathcal{G}}+8{\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}}{\partial^{2}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}\right)}{8({\partial^{2}\mathcal{G}})^{3}}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{4{(\partial^{4}\mathcal{G}})^{2}+30({\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}})^{2}{\partial^{2}\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}+24(\partial^{4}{\mathcal{G}})^{2}({\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}})^{2}+7{\partial^{5}\mathcal{G}}{\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}}+32{\partial^{4}\mathcal{G}}{\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}}{\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}}{48({\partial^{2}\mathcal{G}})^{4}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{30({\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}})^{3}{\partial\mathcal{F}^{(1)}}+25{\partial^{4}\mathcal{G}}({\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}})^{2}}{48({\partial^{2}\mathcal{G}})^{5}}+\tfrac{5({\partial^{3}\mathcal{G}})^{4}}{16{(\partial^{2}\mathcal{G}})^{6}}\,,

where
\partial\equiv\partial_{T}
evaluated at the extremum of

\mathcal{G}(T,T^{\prime})
with respect to
T
, assuming that this extremum is unique.

4.2
Relative conifold monodromy

In order to derive the modular properties of the generating series of GW and PT invariants introduced in §
2.4
, we shall exploit the wave-function transformation property of
Z_{\rm top}
under a certain monodromy
U
in Kähler moduli space
\mathcal{M}
, which acts on the fiber modulus as

T\mapsto\frac{T}{1+NT}
, while preserving the large base limit
S^{\alpha}\to\mathrm{i}\infty
. Together
with the large volume monodromy
T\mapsto T+1
, this generates a subgroup of
\Gamma_{1}(N)
which coincides with
\Gamma_{1}(N)
for
N\leq 4
.

Generalizing
[
49
]
to the case of genus one fibrations with an
N
-section, it turns out that the relevant monodromy
U
corresponds to an auto-equivalence
g_{U}
of the derived category of coherent sheaves
\mathcal{C}=D^{b}\Coh X
given by a Fourier-Mukai transformation with respect to the ideal sheaf of the relative diagonal in the fiber product
X\times_{B}X

[
33
, Section 3.3]
.
Its action on the Chern character of a brane
\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}\in\mathcal{C}
is given by

\displaystyle g_{U}:\,\text{ch}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet})\mapsto\text{ch}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet})-\pi_{2,*}\left[\pi_{1}^{*}\left(\text{ch}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet})\text{Td}(T_{X/B})\right)\right]\,,

(4.22)

where
\text{Td}(T_{X/B})
is the Todd class of the virtual relative tangent bundle of the fibration.
In Appendix
B
, we use this relation to compute the action of
U
on the integral basis
of the charge lattice
\Gamma

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}=\left(\text{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{X}),\,\text{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{D_{e}}),\,\text{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{D_{\alpha}}),\,\text{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}),\,\text{ch}(\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}),\,\text{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{\text{pt.}})\,\right)^{T}\,.

(4.23)

The end result then takes the form
\mathcal{B}\mapsto U\mathcal{B}
where
U
is the matrix with integer coefficients
21
21

21

The integrality of
\frac{N}{2}\left(a_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{N}\ell_{\gamma}\right)C^{\gamma\beta}
is not obvious but implied by the fact that the Fourier-Mukai transform induces an automorphism of
H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Z})
.

\displaystyle U=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}1&0&-c^{\beta}&\frac{N}{2}c^{\gamma}(a_{\gamma}-C_{\gamma\gamma})&0_{\beta}&0\\
-N&1&\frac{N}{2}\left(a_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{N}\ell_{\gamma}\right)C^{\gamma\beta}&\rho&0_{\beta}&0\\
0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&\delta^{\beta}_{\alpha}&-Nc_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha\beta}&0_{\alpha}\\
0&0&0^{\beta}&1&0_{\beta}&0\\
0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&-C^{\alpha\beta}&\frac{N}{2}C^{\alpha\gamma}\left(a_{\gamma}-C_{\gamma\gamma}\right)&\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}&0^{\alpha}\\
0&0&0^{\beta}&-N&0_{\beta}&1\end{array}\right)\,,

with
C_{\gamma\gamma}
being the vector of diagonal entries of the matrix
C_{\alpha\beta}
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\rho=N^{2}+\frac{N}{4}(\ell_{\alpha}+Na_{\alpha})C^{\alpha\beta}(C_{\beta\beta}-a_{\beta})-\frac{N}{12}(2\kappa+c_{2})\,.\end{split}

(4.30)

If the base
B
is a del Pezzo surface, then
C_{\alpha\alpha}-a_{\alpha}=-2
and we find the simpler expression

\displaystyle\rho=-N\left[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\alpha=1}^{b_{2}(B)}(Na^{\alpha}+\ C^{\alpha\beta}\ell_{\beta})-N+\frac{1}{6}\kappa+\frac{1}{12}c_{2}\right]\,.

(4.31)

The combination
\frac{1}{6}\kappa+\frac{1}{12}c_{2}
is equal to the holomorphic Euler characteristic of the divisor
D_{e}
, so is integer, and one may check that
\sum_{\alpha}(Na^{\alpha}+\ C^{\alpha\beta}\ell_{\beta})
is always even. Using
\check{\mathcal{F}}^{(0)}:=(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{-3}\mathcal{F}^{(0)}
, we introduce the period vector
22
22

22

This differs from the vector
V=(F_{\Lambda},X^{\Lambda})
in (
2.2
) by a basis change.

V=X^{0}(1,T,S^{\alpha},-(2\check{\mathcal{F}}^{(0)}-T\partial_{T}\check{\mathcal{F}}^{(0)}-S^{\alpha}\partial_{S^{\alpha}}\check{\mathcal{F}}^{(0)}),-\partial_{T}\check{\mathcal{F}}^{(0)},-\partial_{S^{\alpha}}\check{\mathcal{F}}^{(0)})^{T}

(4.32)

associated to the prepotential (in ‘primed’ basis, see footnote
5
)

\mathcal{F}^{(0)}(T,S^{\alpha})=(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\left(\frac{\kappa T^{3}}{6}+\frac{T^{2}}{2}\ell_{\alpha}S^{\alpha}+\frac{NT}{2}S^{\alpha}C_{\alpha\beta}S^{\beta}\right)+f^{(0)}(S^{\alpha},T)

(4.33)

where

f^{(0)}(T,S^{\alpha})=\sum_{k_{\alpha}\geq 0,n\geq 0}{\rm GW}_{k_{\alpha}C^{\alpha}+nF}^{(0)}e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}(nT+k_{\alpha}S^{\alpha})},

(4.34)

where the constant map contribution is as usual
{\rm GW}_{0,0_{\alpha}}^{(0)}=-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{X}\zeta(3)
.
In terms of this basis, the monodromy acts as
V\mapsto U^{\prime}V
with
23
23

23

Here we used the conjectural equality
a_{\alpha}=c_{\alpha}
. Note that
U^{\prime}
is not integral in general, unlike
U
.

U^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}1&N&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\\
0&1&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\\
\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha}&\frac{N}{2}c^{\alpha}&\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}&0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&-C^{\alpha\beta}\\
\frac{12-\chi_{B}}{4}&\frac{N(12-\chi_{B})}{4}&0^{\beta}&1&0&-\frac{1}{2}c^{\beta}\\
-\frac{N(12-\chi_{B})}{4}&\frac{(\chi_{B}-8)N^{2}}{4}-\frac{c_{2}N}{12}&-\frac{N}{2}c_{\alpha}&-N&1&\frac{N}{2}c^{\beta}\\
0_{\alpha}&-\frac{N}{2}c_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha\beta}&0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(4.35)

The two matrices are related as
U=PU^{\prime}P^{-1}
where

P=\begin{pmatrix}0&\frac{c_{2}}{24}&\frac{1}{2}c_{\beta}&1&0&0_{\beta}\\
-\frac{\kappa}{6}-\frac{c_{2}}{24}&-\frac{\kappa}{2}&-\frac{1}{2}\ell_{\beta}&0&1&0_{\beta}\\
-\frac{1}{2}c_{\alpha}&N_{\alpha}-\frac{N}{2}c_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha\beta}&0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&\delta_{\alpha\beta}\\
0&1&0_{\alpha}&0&0&0_{\beta}\\
0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&\delta_{\alpha\beta}&0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha\beta}\\
-1&0&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\end{pmatrix}

(4.36)

with
N_{\alpha}
being a vector with all entries equal to
N
.

Using (
4.35
), we find that the relative conifold monodromy acts on
(T,S^{\alpha})
by

\begin{split}T\mapsto&\frac{T}{1+NT},\quad S^{\alpha}\mapsto S^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha}+\frac{C^{\alpha\beta}}{1+NT}\left(\frac{T^{2}}{2}\ell_{\beta}+\frac{\partial_{S^{\beta}}f^{(0)}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\right)\\
\end{split}

(4.37)

or in terms of the shifted variable
\widehat{S}_{\alpha}
defined in (
2.43
),

\widehat{S}^{\alpha}\mapsto\widehat{S}^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}\frac{C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{S^{\beta}}f^{(0)}}{1+NT}

(4.38)

while the (instanton part of the) prepotential
f^{(0)}(S^{\alpha},T)
should transform as

\begin{split}f^{(0)}(T,S^{\alpha})\mapsto&\frac{f^{(0)}(T,S^{\alpha})}{(1+NT)^{2}}+\frac{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}{(1+NT)^{2}}\left[\frac{\widehat{\kappa}NT^{4}}{6(1+NT)}+\frac{NT^{2}}{24}\left(\widehat{c}_{2}-12N\right)\right.\\
&\left.-\frac{12-\chi_{B}}{8}(1+NT)\right]+\frac{1}{2(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}(1+NT)^{3}}C_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{S^{\alpha}}f^{(0)}\partial_{S^{\beta}}f^{(0)}\,.\end{split}

(4.39)

In particular, it follows that the derivative of
f^{(0)}
with respect to
S_{\alpha}
(or, equivalently, with respect to
\widehat{S}_{\alpha}
) should transform like a modular form of weight
-2
under
\Gamma_{1}(N)
,

\partial_{S^{\alpha}}f^{(0)}=\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}f^{(0)}\mapsto\frac{\partial_{S^{\alpha}}f^{(0)}}{(1+NT)^{2}}\,.

(4.40)

It will be convenient to introduce

\widehat{f}^{(0)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha}):=\frac{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}{6}\widehat{\kappa}T^{3}+f^{(0)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})

(4.41)

whose transformation property is somewhat simpler than (
4.39
),

\begin{split}\widehat{f}^{(0)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})\mapsto&\frac{\widehat{f}^{(0)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})}{(1+NT)^{2}}+\frac{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}{(1+NT)^{2}}\left[\frac{NT^{2}}{24}\left(\widehat{c}_{2}-12N\right)-\frac{12-\chi_{B}}{8}(1+NT)\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{2(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}(1+NT)^{3}}C_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}\widehat{f}^{(0)}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}\widehat{f}^{(0)}\,.\end{split}

(4.42)

Lift of a general
\Gamma_{1}(N)
element

More generally, any element
\gamma=\scriptsize\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}\in\Gamma_{1}(N)
can be obtained by suitable product of large volume monodromies
T\mapsto T+1
, relative conifold monodromies
T\mapsto T/(NT+1)

and monodromies around other components of the discriminant locus when
N>4
.
The corresponding
element
g_{\gamma}\in Sp(2b_{2}(X)+2,\mathds{Z})
in general acts on
T,S^{\alpha}
via transformations of the form

\begin{split}T&\mapsto\frac{aT+b}{cT+d},\quad\\
S^{\alpha}&\mapsto S^{\alpha}+\frac{\ell^{\alpha}[1+(cT-a)(cT+d)]}{2Nc(cT+d)}+\frac{c}{N(cT+d)}\frac{C_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}\widehat{f}^{(0)}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}+\delta^{\alpha},\\
\widehat{f}^{(0)}&(\widehat{S}^{\alpha},T)\mapsto\frac{\widehat{f}^{(0)}(\widehat{S}^{\alpha},T)}{(cT+d)^{2}}+\frac{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\left(xT^{2}+yT+z\right)}{(cT+d)^{2}}+\frac{C_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}\widehat{f}^{(0)}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}\widehat{f}^{(0)}}{2(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}(cT+d)^{3}}\end{split}

(4.43)

for some
\gamma
-dependent constants
\delta^{\alpha},x,y,z
subject to the group relations.
While the transformation of
S^{\alpha}
looks complicated, it is such that
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
, defined in (
2.43
), transforms as

\widehat{S}^{\alpha}\mapsto\widehat{S}^{\alpha}+\frac{c}{N(cT+d)}\frac{C_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}\widehat{f}^{(0)}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}+\delta^{\alpha}.

(4.44)

and the variable
\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}
defined in (
4.54
) below transforms in an even simpler way,
\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}\mapsto\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}+\delta^{\alpha}
.
This product of monodromies is realized by an auto-equivalence
g_{\gamma}\in{\rm Aut}(\mathcal{C})

acting on the period vector (
4.32
) by
V\mapsto U^{\prime}_{\gamma}V
, where

U^{\prime}_{\gamma}
is a matrix of the form

U^{\prime}_{\gamma}=\begin{pmatrix}d&c&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\\
b&a&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\\
d\delta^{\alpha}-\frac{b\ell^{\alpha}}{2N}&\frac{\ell^{\alpha}(d-a)}{2N}+c\delta^{\alpha}&d\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}&0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&-\frac{c}{N}C^{\alpha\beta}\\
\rho_{1}&\rho_{2}&b\delta^{\beta}N&a&-b&\frac{b\ell^{\beta}}{2N}-a\delta^{\beta}\\
\rho_{3}&\rho_{4}&-\frac{b\ell_{\alpha}}{2}-d\delta_{\alpha}N&-c&d&\frac{\ell^{\beta}(a-d)}{2N}+c\delta^{\beta}\\
-b\delta_{\alpha}N&-a\delta_{\alpha}N-\frac{b\ell_{\alpha}}{2}&-bN\delta_{\alpha\beta}&0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&a\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}\end{pmatrix}

(4.45)

where

\begin{split}\rho_{1}=&b\left(y+\frac{\delta^{2}N}{2}\right)-2az\,,\\
\rho_{2}=&\frac{1}{2}\left(a\delta^{2}N-2ay+4bx+b\delta\ell\right)\,,\\
\rho_{3}=&\frac{1}{2}\left(-b\delta\cdot\ell+4cz-d\left(\delta^{2}N+2y\right)\right)\,,\\
\rho_{4}=&-\frac{2N\left(a\delta\cdot\ell+c\delta^{2}N-2cy+4dx+d\delta\cdot\ell\right)+b\ell^{2}}{4N}\end{split}

(4.46)

and
\delta^{2}=\delta_{\alpha}\delta^{\beta},\delta\cdot\ell=\delta_{\alpha}\ell^{\alpha}
, etc. For
\gamma=\scriptsize\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
, one recovers the standard action of the large volume monodromy
T\mapsto T+1
,

U^{\prime}_{T}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\\
1&1&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\\
0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}&0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha\beta}\\
\frac{\kappa}{6}&\frac{\kappa}{2}&\frac{\ell^{\beta}}{2}&1&-1&0^{\beta}\\
-\frac{\kappa}{2}&-\kappa&-\ell_{\alpha}&0&1&0^{\beta}\\
-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}}{2}&-\ell_{\alpha}&-NC_{\alpha\beta}&0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}\\
\end{pmatrix}

(4.47)

corresponding to
\delta^{\alpha}=\frac{\ell^{\alpha}}{2N},(x,y,z)=(\widehat{\kappa}/2,\widehat{\kappa}/2,\tilde{\kappa}/6)
.
While we do not know how to determine the constants
\delta^{\alpha},x,y,z
for a general group element, an important constraint is that the matrix

U_{\gamma}=PU^{\prime}_{\gamma}P^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}a&-b&*&*&*&*\\
-c&d&*&*&*&*\\
0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&a\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}&*&-bNC_{\alpha\beta}&*\\
0&0&0^{\beta}&a&0_{\beta}&-b\\
0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&-\frac{c}{N}C^{\alpha\beta}&*&d\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}&*\\
0&0&0^{\beta}&-c&0_{\beta}&d\end{array}\right)\,,

(4.48)

where the stars stand for somewhat complicated but easily computed combinations of
x,y,z,\delta,\kappa,c_{\alpha},c_{2}
,
should have integer entries.

4.3
Modularity of genus 0 GW invariants

At this point, we can deduce the modular properties of generating series of GW invariants (
2.44
) at genus zero, by inserting the Fourier expansion

\widehat{f}^{(0)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})=\frac{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}{6}\widehat{\kappa}T^{3}+\sum_{k_{\alpha}\geq 0}f^{(0)}_{k_{\alpha}}(T)\,e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k_{\alpha}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}

(4.49)

into (
4.42
). As before, we use the notations
f^{(0)}_{k_{\alpha}}=f^{(0)}_{H}
interchangeably,
for any nef divisor class
H=k_{\alpha}\check{D}^{\alpha}
on
B
, and in this section omit the genus superscript
(0)
to lighten the notation.

First we note that in the large base limit
S^{\alpha}\to\mathrm{i}\infty
, the quadratic term on the second line of (
4.39
) is exponentially suppressed, so we get the transformation property for the base degree
0_{\alpha}
, genus zero generating series

\begin{split}(1+NT)^{2}\widehat{f}_{0_{\alpha}}\left(\tfrac{T}{1+NT}\right)-\widehat{f}_{0_{\alpha}}(T)=(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\left(\tfrac{NT^{2}}{24}\left(\widehat{c}_{2}-12N\right)-\tfrac{12-\chi_{B}}{8}(1+NT)\right)\end{split}

(4.50)

in perfect agreement with (
3.32
).
In particular, the Yukawa coupling

Y_{0}(T)=(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\partial^{3}_{T}\widehat{f}_{0_{\alpha}}
is a modular form of weight
4
under
\Gamma_{1}(N)
,
and
\widehat{f}_{0_{\alpha}}(T)
is its holomorphic Eichler integral.

We now turn to the case of non-vanishing base degree,
k_{\alpha}>0
. In this case, we can
determine the modular properties of the generating series
f_{k_{\alpha}}(T)
by inserting
(
4.49
) into (
4.40
),

\sum_{k_{\alpha}>0}k_{\alpha}\,f_{k_{\alpha}}(\tfrac{T}{1+NT})\,e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k_{\alpha}(\widehat{S}^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha}+\frac{C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f}{1+NT})}=\frac{1}{(1+NT)^{2}}\sum_{k_{\alpha}>0}k_{\alpha}\,f_{k_{\alpha}}(T)\,e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k_{\alpha}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}

(4.51)

and expanding in powers of
e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}
on both sides. The only difficulty is that
the function
\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f
appearing in the exponent on the left-hand side is itself
a function of
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
which needs to be expanded.
24
24

24

Such doubly exponential contributions were ignored in
[
33
]
, but in fact,
e^{-e^{-S}}=1+e^{-S}+\mathcal{O}(e^{-2S})
and such effects are key for the modular properties of the topological string amplitude.

For primitive curve classes, this
complication does not arise and one immediately concludes that the generating series of genus 0 GV invariants is a modular form of weight
-2
with multiplier system,

f_{k_{\alpha}}(\tfrac{T}{1+NT})=\frac{e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi k_{\alpha}c^{\alpha}}f_{k_{\alpha}}(T)}{(1+NT)^{2}},\quad f_{k_{\alpha}}(T+1)=e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}\pi}{N}k_{\alpha}\ell^{\alpha}}f_{k_{\alpha}}(T)\qquad\mbox{(primitive case)}

(4.52)

where
\ell^{\alpha}:=C^{\alpha\beta}\ell_{\alpha}
.
In general however, there are contributions from lower degrees, leading to a modular anomaly of the form

e^{\mathrm{i}\pi k_{\alpha}c^{\alpha}}(1+NT)^{2}f_{H}(\tfrac{T}{1+NT})-f_{H}(T)=\frac{1}{1+NT}\sum_{H=\sum H_{i}}a_{\{H_{i}\}}\prod_{i}\frac{f_{H_{i}}(T)}{1+NT}

(4.53)

where
a_{\{k_{i}\}}
are rational coefficients (see (
4.65
) below for some examples). This indicates that
f_{H}
is in general a quasimodular form of weight
-2
.

To show this more explicitly, it is useful to introduce yet another shifted version of the Kähler moduli
S^{\alpha}
, namely

\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}:=\widehat{S}^{\alpha}-\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{12}\frac{C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f^{(0)}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}

(4.54)

where
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
was defined in (
2.43
) and
E_{2}(T)
is the quasimodular Eisenstein series of weight 2 already encountered in §
3.1
.
Using

E_{2}\left(\frac{NT}{1+NT}\right)=(1+NT)^{2}E_{2}(NT)+\frac{12}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}(1+NT)

(4.55)

which follows from (
3.1
),
we see that
\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}
transforms simply with a constant shift under the relative conifold monodromy,

\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}\mapsto\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha}\,.

(4.56)

Thus, upon Fourier expanding
\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}f
as a function of
\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}
rather than

\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
,

\sum_{k_{\alpha}>0}k_{\alpha}\,f_{k_{\alpha}}(T)\,e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k_{\beta}\widehat{S}^{\beta}}=\sum_{k_{\alpha}>0}k_{\alpha}\,\widetilde{f}_{k_{\alpha}}(T)\,e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k_{\beta}\widetilde{S}^{\beta}}

(4.57)

the new generating series
\widetilde{f}_{k_{\alpha}}(T)
(which are polynomials in
E_{2}(NT)
and in the previous
series
f_{k_{\alpha}}(T)
) are now actual modular forms of weight
-2
with a multiplier system but no modular anomaly,

\widetilde{f}_{k_{\alpha}}(\tfrac{T}{1+NT})=\frac{e^{-\mathrm{i}\pi k_{\alpha}c^{\alpha}}\widetilde{f}_{k_{\alpha}}(T)}{(1+NT)^{2}}\,,\quad\widetilde{f}_{k_{\alpha}}(T+1)=e^{-\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{N}k_{\alpha}\ell^{\alpha}}\widetilde{f}_{k_{\alpha}}(T)\,.

(4.58)

In fact, one may integrate the vectorial equation (
4.57
) to a scalar equation

\widetilde{f}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},T):=f(T,S^{\alpha})-\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{24(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}f\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f=\sum_{k_{\alpha}\geq 0}\widetilde{f}_{k_{\alpha}}(T)\,e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k_{\alpha}\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}}\,.

(4.59)

To see that the last equality defines the same Fourier modes
\tilde{f}_{k_{\alpha}>0}

as in (
4.57
), along with the zero mode
\tilde{f}_{0_{\alpha}}(T)
, we use that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial\tilde{f}}{\partial\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}}=&\frac{\partial\widehat{S}^{\beta}}{\partial\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\widehat{S}^{\beta}}\left(f(T,S^{\alpha})-\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{24(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}C^{\gamma\delta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\gamma}}f\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\delta}}f\right)\\
=&\frac{\partial\widehat{S}^{\beta}}{\partial\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}}\left(\delta_{\beta}^{\gamma}-C^{\gamma\delta}\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{12}\frac{\partial^{2}_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}\widehat{S}^{\delta}}f}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\right)\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\gamma}}f\,,\end{split}

(4.60)

together with

\displaystyle\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}\widetilde{S}^{\beta}=\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}-C^{\gamma\beta}\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{12}\frac{\partial^{2}_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}\widehat{S}^{\gamma}}f}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\,.

(4.61)

From this follows the important fact that

\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}f=\partial_{\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}}\widetilde{f}
.
Taking derivatives with respect to
E_{2}(NT)
on both sides of the first equality in (
4.59
), keeping
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
fixed, we get

-\frac{1}{12(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f\,\partial_{\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}}\widetilde{f}=\partial_{E_{2}(NT)}f-\frac{1}{24(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f\,\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}f

(4.62)

and therefore

\partial_{E_{2}(NT)}f=-\frac{1}{24(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}f\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f\,

(4.63)

Further expanding in Fourier modes with respect to
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
, we get the genus zero version of (
2.55
),

\partial_{E_{2}(NT)}f_{H}^{(0)}(T)=-\frac{1}{24}\sum_{H=H_{1}+H_{2}}(H_{1}\cap H_{2})f_{H_{1}}^{(0)}f_{H_{2}}^{(0)}

(4.64)

confirming that the generating series
f_{H}^{(0)}(T)
(now restoring the genus superscript) are quasimodular forms of weight
-2
, and depth given by the maximal number of elements in possible decompositions of
H=\sum H_{i}
into a sum of effective curve classes in
H_{2}(B,\mathds{Z})
.
In contrast, the functions
\widetilde{f}_{H}^{(0)}(T)
defined by (
4.59
) are ordinary holomorphic modular forms of weight
-2
under

\Gamma_{1}(N)
, given by the depth zero part of
f_{H}^{(0)}(T)
.

Explicit formulae for genus one fibrations on
\mathds{P}^{2}

To illustrate the constructions above, let us consider genus one fibrations with
N
-section on
\mathds{P}^{2}
. For brevity we write
f_{k}(T):=f_{kH}(T)
, where
H
is the hyperplane
class of
\mathds{P}^{2}
. Denoting by
A_{k}
the left-hand side of (
4.53
), and setting

\hat{f}_{k_{i}}=f_{k_{i}}/(1+NT)
, we find the following anomalous transformations properties of the generating series of genus zero GW invariants,

\begin{split}A_{1}=&0\,,\\
A_{2}=&-\frac{1}{2}\hat{f}_{1}^{2}\,,\\
A_{3}=&-\frac{1}{3}\hat{f}_{1}^{3}+2\hat{f}_{1}\hat{f}_{2}\,,\\
A_{4}=&-\frac{2}{3}\hat{f}_{1}^{4}+4\hat{f}_{1}^{2}\hat{f}_{2}-2\hat{f}_{2}^{2}-3\hat{f}_{1}\hat{f}_{3}\,,\\
A_{5}=&-\frac{25}{24}\hat{f}_{1}^{5}+\frac{25}{3}\hat{f}_{2}\hat{f}_{1}^{3}-\frac{15}{2}\hat{f}_{3}\hat{f}_{1}^{2}-10\hat{f}_{2}^{2}\hat{f}_{1}+4\hat{f}_{4}\hat{f}_{1}+6\hat{f}_{2}\hat{f}_{3}\,.\end{split}

(4.65)

In contrast, the combinations
\widetilde{f}_{k}
defined by (
4.57
) transform as weakly holomorphic modular forms of weight
-2
,

\begin{split}\widetilde{f}_{1}=&f_{1}\,\\
\widetilde{f}_{2}=&f_{2}+\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{24}f_{1}^{2}\,,\\
\widetilde{f}_{3}=&f_{3}+\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{6}f_{1}f_{2}+\frac{E_{2}(NT)^{2}}{288}f_{1}^{3}\,,\\
\widetilde{f}_{4}=&f_{4}+\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{4}f_{1}f_{3}+\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{6}f_{2}^{2}+\frac{E_{2}(NT)^{2}}{36}f_{1}^{2}f_{2}+\frac{E_{2}(NT)^{3}}{2592}f_{1}^{4}\,,\\
\widetilde{f}_{5}=&f_{5}+\frac{25E_{2}^{4}}{497664}f_{1}^{5}+\frac{25E_{2}^{3}}{5184}f_{2}f_{1}^{3}+\frac{5}{96}E_{2}^{2}f_{3}f_{1}^{2}+\frac{5}{72}E_{2}^{2}f_{2}^{2}f_{1}+\frac{1}{3}E_{2}f_{4}f_{1}+\frac{1}{2}E_{2}f_{2}f_{3}\end{split}

(4.66)

where in the last line, we left the argument
NT
of
E_{2}
implicit. Inverting this triangular system of equations, it is straightforward to check that the
f_{k}
’s satisfy the holomorphic anomaly equations

\frac{\partial}{\partial E_{2}(NT)}f_{k}(T)=-\frac{1}{24}\sum_{k=k_{1}+k_{2}}f_{k_{1}}(T)f_{k_{2}}(T)\,.

(4.67)

In fact, the
\widetilde{f}_{k}
’s are simply the depth zero part of the quasimodular forms
f_{k}
.
Using the ansatz

\widetilde{f}_{k}(T)=\frac{[\Delta_{2N}(T)]^{\frac{k(3N^{2}+\ell)}{2N}}}{\eta^{36k}(NT)}P_{18k-2-k(3N^{2}+\ell)}(T)\,,

(4.68)

where
\Delta_{2N}(T)
is the weight
2N
modular form defined in (
2.53
),
we can easily determine the holomorphic modular form
P_{w}
of weight
w
under
\Gamma_{1}(N)

from the knowledge of the GV invariants
{\rm GV}_{k_{1},k_{2}\leq k}
for sufficiently large
k_{1}
.
The power of
\Delta_{2N}
in (
4.68
) is the lowest one allowed by (
2.54
),
although it can be frequently increased by an integer by lowering the weight
of
P_{w}
correspondingly. In the simplest case of the elliptic fibration over
\mathds{P}^{2}
, with
N=1
, the
corresponding modular forms were already identified up to degree 3 in
[
25
, §D.2]

[
26
, (5.15)]
, which we record below:

\begin{split}\widetilde{f}_{1}=&\frac{31E_{4}^{4}+113E_{4}E_{6}^{2}}{48\eta^{36}}\\
\widetilde{f}_{2}=&-\frac{196319E_{4}E_{6}^{5}+755906E_{4}^{4}E_{6}^{3}+208991E_{4}^{7}E_{6}}{221184\eta^{72}}\\
\widetilde{f}_{3}=&\tfrac{E_{4}(49789907821E_{4}^{12}+1904214859592E_{4}^{9}E_{6}^{2}+6966210848730E_{4}^{6}E_{6}^{4}+4311836724416E_{4}^{3}E_{6}^{6}+360744024241E_{6}^{8})}{557256278016\eta^{108}}\end{split}

(4.69)

Similar expressions for genus-one fibrations with
N
-section over
\mathds{P}^{2}
are collected in Appendix §
D
.
In order to extract from these expressions the genus zero GV invariants, it is necessary to invert
the system (
4.66
) so as to express
f_{k}
in terms of
\widetilde{f}_{k}
, and then use the multicover formula

f^{{\rm GV}}_{k}(T)=\sum_{d|k}\frac{\mu(d)}{d^{3}}f_{k/d}(dT)

(4.70)

where
\mu(d)
is the Möbius function, equal to
(-1)^{k}
if
d
is the product of
k
distinct primes, or zero otherwise. For
N=1
, this produces

\displaystyle f_{1}^{{\rm GV}}(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{1}(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-3/2}\left(3-1080\mbox{q}+143770\mbox{q}^{2}+204071184\mbox{q}^{3}+21772947555\mbox{q}^{4}+\dots\right)\,,

\displaystyle f_{2}^{{\rm GV}}(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{2}(T)-\frac{E_{2}(T)}{24}\widetilde{f}_{1}^{2}(T)-\frac{1}{8}f_{1}(2T)

(4.71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-3}\left(-6+2700\mbox{q}-574560\mbox{q}^{2}+74810520\mbox{q}^{3}-49933059660\mbox{q}^{4}+\dots\right)\,,

\displaystyle f_{3}^{{\rm GV}}(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{3}(T)-\frac{1}{3^{3}}\widetilde{f}_{1}(3T)-\frac{E_{2}(T)}{6}\widetilde{f}_{1}\widetilde{f}_{2}+\frac{E_{2}^{2}(T)}{2\cdot(24)^{2}}\widetilde{f}_{1}^{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{q}^{-9/2}\left(27-17280\mbox{q}+5051970\mbox{q}^{2}-913383000\mbox{q}^{3}+224108858700\mbox{q}^{4}+\dots\right)\,.

For
N\geq 1
, the same expressions hold (but not the
q
-expansions) after rescaling the argument of
E_{2}
by a factor of
N
.

4.4
Modularity at higher genus from the wave-function property

We now turn to the modular properties of generating series of higher genus GW invariants, and for this purpose study the transformation of the topological string partition function
Z_{\rm top}
under the relative conifold monodromy.

4.4.1
Modular properties of
Z_{\rm top}

As reviewed in §
4.1
, under a general monodromy, the topological string partition function transforms according to the metaplectic representation (
4.19
).
Now, since the upper-right block in the matrix
U^{\prime}
(
4.35
) is not invertible, the definition (
4.11
) of the kernel
\mathcal{S}
cannot apply literally. This is simply due to the fact that the transformation
T\mapsto T/(1+NT)
does not mix
T
with the conjugate variables

\displaystyle(\mathcal{F}_{0},\mathcal{F}_{T},\mathcal{F}_{S^{\alpha}}):=(2\mathcal{F}^{(0)}-T\partial_{T}\mathcal{F}^{(0)}-S^{\alpha}\partial_{S^{\alpha}}\mathcal{F}^{(0)},\partial_{T}\mathcal{F}^{(0)},\partial_{S^{\alpha}}\mathcal{F}^{(0)})\,.

(4.72)

The remedy, already hinted at below (
4.11
), is that the Gaussian kernel should be restricted
to the variables
S^{\alpha}
, while the transformation of
T
is enforced by a Dirac delta function

\delta(T^{\prime}-T/(1+NT))
. The appropriate kernel
\mathcal{S}(S^{\alpha},S^{\prime\alpha})
can be found by requiring that the prepotential transforms as in (
2.4
),

(1+NT)^{2}\,\mathcal{F}^{\prime(0)}(T^{\prime},S^{\prime\alpha})=\langle\mathcal{F}^{(0)}(T,S^{\alpha})-\mathcal{S}(S^{\alpha},S^{\prime\alpha})\rangle_{S^{\alpha}}

(4.73)

where the bracket denotes extremization with respect to
S^{\alpha}
, and

T^{\prime},S^{\prime\alpha}
on the l.h.s. are the transformed variables (
4.37
). The factor
of
1+NT
on the l.h.s. follows from the relation
X^{\prime 0}=cX^{1}+dX^{0}=X^{0}(cT+d)
, which
implies that the topological string coupling
\lambda=1/X^{0}
should transform as

\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{\prime}=\frac{\lambda}{1+NT}\,.

(4.74)

Moreover, the value
of
S^{\alpha}
extremizing (
4.73
) should be consistent with (
4.37
).
This determines uniquely the quadratic kernel

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}=&(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\left(-\frac{1}{2}(S^{\alpha}-(1+NT)S^{\prime\alpha})C_{\alpha\beta}(S^{\beta}-(1+NT)S^{\prime\beta}+c^{\beta})\right.\\
&\left.-\tfrac{NT}{2}S^{\alpha}C_{\alpha\beta}c^{\beta}+\tfrac{12N^{2}-c_{2}N}{24}T^{2}\right)\,.\end{split}

(4.75)

According to the prescription(
4.19
) in §
4.1
, the topological string partition function
should thus transform as

Z_{\rm top}(T^{\prime},S^{\prime\alpha},\lambda^{\prime})=\int\,e^{\frac{-\mathcal{S}(S^{\alpha},S^{\prime\alpha})}{\lambda^{2}}}\,Z_{\rm top}(T,S^{\alpha},\lambda)\,\prod_{\alpha=1}^{b_{2}(S)}\frac{\mathrm{d}S^{\alpha}}{\lambda}\,.

(4.76)

Inserting the genus expansion in (
2.7
) and trading
S^{\alpha}
for the shifted
variable
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
defined in (
2.43
), we get

\displaystyle\exp\left(\sum_{g\geq 0}\lambda^{\prime 2g-2}F^{\prime(g)}(T^{\prime},\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha})\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{(1+NT)^{\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}-1}}{\lambda^{b_{2}(B)}}\int\prod\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\mathcal{S}}{\lambda^{2}}+\sum_{g\geq 0}\lambda^{2g-2}F^{(g)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})\right)

(4.77)

where, on the r.h.s, the
\mathcal{O}(1/\lambda^{2})
terms are given by

\begin{split}\mathcal{F}^{(0)}-\mathcal{S}=&(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\left(\frac{1+NT}{2}\left[(\widehat{S}^{\alpha}-\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha})C_{\alpha\beta}(\widehat{S}^{\beta}-\widehat{S}^{\prime\beta}+c^{\beta})+NT\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha}C_{\alpha\beta}\widehat{S}^{\prime\beta}\right]\right.\\
&\left.+\frac{\widehat{\kappa}T^{3}}{6}-T^{2}\frac{12N^{2}-\widehat{c}_{2}N}{24}\right)+f^{(0)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})\,.\end{split}

(4.78)

Computing this integral in perturbation theory around the saddle point
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}=\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha}-\frac{C^{\alpha\beta}}{1+NT}\frac{\partial_{S^{\alpha}}f^{(0)}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}}
determines the transformed free energies
F^{\prime(g)}(T^{\prime},S^{\prime\alpha})
in terms of the original ones.

In the classical, leading order approximation, we recover by construction the transformation property of
f^{(0)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})
discussed in the previous subsection. At one-loop order around the saddle point, we get a factor

\frac{(\lambda^{2}/(1+NT))^{b_{2}(B)/2}}{\sqrt{\,{\rm det}\,\left(C_{\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}(1+NT)}\partial^{2}_{S^{\alpha}S^{\beta}}f^{(0)}\right)}}\,,

(4.79)

up to an overall constant factor,
from the fluctuation determinant. Using the fact that
b_{2}(B)=\chi_{B}-2
for a rational surface, and including the contribution from the prefactor
\lambda^{\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}-1}
in
Z_{\rm top.}
, we obtain that the genus one amplitude

\widehat{f}^{(1)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha}):=-\frac{\widehat{c}_{2}}{24}(2\pi\mathrm{i})T+f^{(1)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})

(4.80)

must transform as

\begin{split}\widehat{f}^{(1)}(T^{\prime},\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha})=&\widehat{f}^{(1)}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha})+\tfrac{\chi_{X}-12\chi_{B}}{24}\log(1+NT)+5\pi{\rm i}-\frac{\pi{\rm i}}{3}\chi_{B}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\log\,{\rm det}\,\left(C_{\alpha\beta}+\tfrac{1}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}(1+NT)}\partial^{2}_{S^{\alpha}S^{\beta}}f^{(0)}\right)\,.\end{split}

(4.81)

Here, we have fixed the constant term using the known transformation property (
3.39
) of the generating function of base degree zero, genus 1
Gromov-Witten invariants, which dominates in the large base limit (where the determinant in (
4.81
) is effectively equal to one).
Unfortunately, we are not able at this stage to derive this constant term directly from the wave function behavior (although we suspect it is related to the choice of steepest descent integration contour). Fourier expanding with respect to
\widehat{S}
, one finds that
the generating series of genus 1 Gromov-Witten invariants at non-zero base degree
H
transforms as a quasimodular form of weight
0
and depth equal to the maximal number of terms in decompositions

H=\sum_{i}H_{i}
into effective divisor classes, plus one.

4.4.2
Introducing the modular covariant wave function
Z_{\rm mod}

In order to characterize the quasimodular properties more precisely, we find it useful to introduce a different polarization, making use of the improved Kähler modulus
\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}
introduced in
(
4.54
), which we recall for the reader’s convenience,

\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}:=\widehat{S}^{\alpha}-\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{12}\frac{C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f^{(0)}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\mapsto\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}+\delta^{\alpha}\,.

(4.82)

Since this definition mixes
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
with the derivative
\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f^{(0)}
, we can view it as a canonical transformation (at fixed
T
), and ask for the wave function in a polarization where
\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}
is diagonalized: since the latter transforms simply by a constant shift under monodromy, we expect that the new wave function will have much simpler transformation properties. With this motivation in mind, we define the topological wave function in ‘modular polarization’ as the transform

Z_{\rm mod}(T,\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},\lambda):=\frac{1}{\left(\lambda\sqrt{E_{2}(NT)}\right)^{b_{2}(B)}}\int Z_{\rm top}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha},\lambda)\,e^{\frac{\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},\widehat{S}^{\alpha})}{\lambda^{2}}}\prod_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}

(4.83)

where
\mathcal{H}
is the quadratic kernel

\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},\widehat{S}^{\alpha})=-(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\frac{NT}{2}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}C_{\alpha\beta}\widehat{S}^{\beta}-(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}\frac{6}{E_{2}(NT)}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}-\widehat{S}^{\alpha})C_{\alpha\beta}(\widetilde{S}^{\beta}-\widehat{S}^{\beta})\,.

(4.84)

The first term in (
4.84
) is chosen to cancel the classical term in the prepotential (
4.33
),
while the second term combines with the instanton contribution to the prepotential
f^{(0)}
such that the integral has a saddle point precisely when (
4.82
) is obeyed. The overall power of
E_{2}(NT)
in (
4.83
) is chosen such the inverse formula takes the same form,

Z_{\rm top}(T,\widehat{S}^{\alpha},\lambda)=\frac{1}{\left(\lambda\sqrt{E_{2}(NT)}\right)^{b_{2}(B)}}\int Z_{\rm mod}(T,\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},\lambda)\,e^{-\frac{\mathcal{H}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},\widehat{S}^{\alpha})}{\lambda^{2}}}\prod_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}\,.

(4.85)

Substituting (
2.7
), we have, more explicitly,

\begin{split}&Z_{\rm mod}(T,\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},\lambda)\\
=&\tfrac{\lambda^{\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}-b_{2}(B)-1}}{E_{2}(NT)^{\frac{1}{2}b_{2}(B)}}\int\exp^{\prime}\left(\frac{\widehat{\kappa}T^{3}}{6\lambda^{2}}-\frac{3C_{\alpha\beta}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}-\widehat{S}^{\alpha})(\widetilde{S}^{\beta}-\widehat{S}^{\beta})}{\pi\mathrm{i}\lambda^{2}E_{2}(NT)}-\frac{\widehat{c}_{2}T+\frac{1}{2}c_{\alpha}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\right)\\
&\times\exp\left(\sum_{g\geq 0}f^{(g)}(\widehat{S}^{\alpha},T)\lambda^{2g-2}\right)\prod_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}\,,\end{split}

(4.86)

where we use
\exp^{\prime}(x)=\exp\left((2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}x\right)
.
To obtain the transformation property of
Z_{\rm mod}
, we need to invert (
4.83
), apply the transformation (
4.76
) and then apply (
4.83
) again.
The result is that
Z_{\rm mod}(\widetilde{S},T,\lambda)

transforms into

\displaystyle Z_{\rm mod}(T^{\prime},\widetilde{S}^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{Z_{\rm mod}(T,\widetilde{S},\lambda)}{\left(\lambda^{3}\sqrt{E_{2}(NT)E_{2}(NT^{\prime})}\right)^{b_{2}(B)}}\,\exp^{\prime}\left(\frac{NT\widehat{S}^{2}}{2\lambda^{2}}+\frac{3(\widetilde{S}-\widehat{S})^{2}}{\pi\mathrm{i}\lambda^{2}E_{2}(NT)}\right)

(4.87)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\exp^{\prime}\left(\tfrac{(\widehat{S}^{\alpha}-(1+NT)\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha})C_{\alpha\beta}(\widehat{S}^{\beta}-(1+NT)\widehat{S}^{\prime\beta}+c^{\beta})}{2\lambda^{2}}+\tfrac{NT}{2\lambda^{2}}c_{\alpha}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}-\tfrac{\left(\frac{N^{2}}{2}-\frac{c_{2}N}{24}\right)T^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\exp^{\prime}\left(-\frac{NT^{\prime}\widehat{S}^{\prime 2}}{2\lambda^{\prime 2}}-\frac{3(\widetilde{S}^{\prime}-\widehat{S}^{\prime})^{2}}{\pi\mathrm{i}\lambda^{\prime 2}E_{2}(NT^{\prime})}\right)\,\prod_{\alpha}\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}\,.

Using that
T^{\prime}=T/(NT+1)
,
\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda/(1+NT)
and the transformation rule (
4.55
) of
E_{2}(NT)
,
one finds that the quadratic from in
(\widehat{S}^{\alpha},\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha})
appearing in the product of exponentials
has half-maximal rank, with null space spanned by
(E_{2}(NT),E_{2}(NT)+\frac{1}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}\tfrac{12}{1+NT})x^{\alpha}
for any
x^{\alpha}\in\mathds{R}^{b_{2}(B)}
. Decomposing

(\widehat{S}^{\alpha},\widehat{S}^{\prime\alpha})=\left((1+NT)E_{2}(NT)x^{\alpha}+y^{\alpha},\left(\tfrac{12}{2\pi\mathrm{i}}+(1+NT)E_{2}(NT)\right)x^{\alpha}+y^{\alpha}\right)

(4.88)

the integral over
x^{\alpha}
leads to a delta function
\delta[(1+NT)(\widetilde{S}^{\prime\alpha}-\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha})/\lambda^{2}]
. Setting
\widetilde{S}^{\prime\alpha}=\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha}
, the remaining Gaussian integral over
y^{\alpha}

is peaked at

\displaystyle y^{\alpha}=\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}-\frac{(1+NT)\left(2\pi\mathrm{i}E_{2}(NT)+12\right)}{24}c^{\alpha}\,,

(4.89)

with Hessian
(1+NT)C_{\alpha\beta}/\left[\lambda^{2}E_{2}(NT)E_{2}(\tfrac{NT}{1+NT})\right]
.
Collecting fluctuation determinants, we get

\begin{split}\frac{Z_{\rm mod}\left(\tfrac{T}{1+NT},\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}+\tfrac{1}{2}c^{\alpha},\tfrac{\lambda}{1+NT}\right)}{Z_{\rm mod}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},T,\lambda)}=&\frac{\exp^{\prime}\left(-\frac{(12N^{2}-c_{2}N)T^{2}}{24\lambda^{2}}+\frac{\chi_{B}-12}{8\lambda^{2}}(1+NT)\right)}{(1+NT)^{\frac{12\chi_{B}-\chi_{X}}{24}}}\,,\end{split}

(4.90)

where we have also used that
\chi_{B}=12-a^{\alpha}a_{\alpha}
and assumed that
a^{\alpha}=c^{\alpha}
. More generally, under a
\Gamma_{1}(N)
monodromy of the form (
4.45
), a similar computation shows that
Z_{\rm mod}
transforms as

\begin{split}Z_{\rm mod}\left(\tfrac{aT+b}{cT+d},\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}+\delta^{\alpha},\tfrac{\lambda}{cT+d}\right)=&(cT+d)^{\frac{\chi_{X}-12\chi_{B}}{24}}\exp^{\prime}\left(\tfrac{xT^{2}+yT+z}{\lambda^{2}}\right)Z_{\rm mod}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},T,\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(4.91)

As a result, if we compute (
4.86
) in perturbation theory around the saddle point and define new topological amplitudes
\widetilde{f}^{(g)}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},T):=\sum_{k_{\alpha}}\widetilde{f}^{(g)}(T)e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}k_{\alpha}\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}}
via

Z_{\rm mod}(T,\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},\lambda)=\lambda^{\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}-1}\exp\left(\frac{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\widehat{\kappa}T^{3}}{6\lambda^{2}}+\pi\mathrm{i}c_{\alpha}\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}+\sum_{g\geq 0}\widetilde{f}^{(g)}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},T)\lambda^{2g-2}\right)

(4.92)

it follows that each Fourier coefficient
\widetilde{f}^{(g)}_{k_{\alpha}}(T)
will be a modular form of weight
2-2g
under
\Gamma_{1}(N)
, with no anomaly except for
g=0,1
. For
g=0
, this definition reproduces (
4.59
), while for
g=1
, it yields

\begin{split}\widetilde{f}^{(1)}(\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},T)=&\widehat{f}^{(1)}(\widehat{S}^{\alpha},T)-\frac{1}{2}\log\,{\rm det}\,\left(C_{\alpha\beta}-\tfrac{E_{2}(NT)}{12(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}\partial^{2}_{S^{\alpha}S^{\beta}}f^{(0)}\right)\\
&-\tfrac{E_{2}(NT)}{12(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}C^{\alpha\beta}c_{\alpha}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f^{(0)}\,.\end{split}

(4.93)

Taking derivatives with respect to
E_{2}(NT)
on both sides of (
4.93
) keeping
\widehat{S}^{\alpha}
fixed, similar to (
4.62
), and using the genus 0 result (
4.63
), we obtain

\begin{split}\partial_{E_{2}(NT)}f^{(1)}=&-\frac{1}{12(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}}f^{(0)}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f^{(1)}\\
&-\frac{1}{24(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{2}}C^{\alpha\beta}\partial^{2}_{\widehat{S}^{\alpha}\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f^{(0)}+\frac{1}{48\pi\mathrm{i}}c_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\widehat{S}^{\beta}}f^{(0)}\end{split}

(4.94)

or in terms of the Fourier coefficients,

\partial_{E_{2}(NT)}f_{H}^{(1)}(T)=-\frac{1}{24}\sum_{H=H_{1}+H_{2}}(H_{1}\cap H_{2})f_{H_{1}}^{(0)}f_{H_{2}}^{(1)}-\frac{1}{24}H\cap(H-c_{1}(B))f_{H}^{(0)}

(4.95)

confirming the quasimodularity of the generating series
f^{(1)}_{H}
.

More generally,
the series
f^{(g)}_{H}
are quasimodular forms of weight
2g-2
and depth equal to
g
plus the maximal number of elements in possible decompositions of
H=\sum_{i}H_{i}
into effective divisor classes, while
the series
\widetilde{f}^{(g)}_{H}
are actual modular forms of weight
2g-2
, equal to the depth zero part of
f^{(g)}_{H}
. See Appendix §
D
for many explicit examples.
We leave it as an exercise to the reader to derive the modular anomaly equations at genus
g>1
using the same techniques.

4.4.3
Jacobi properties of
Z_{H}(T,\lambda)

We are now ready to derive the Jacobi property of the normalized generating series of PT invariants defined in
(
2.47
). Substituting (
2.46
) in (
4.83
) and
integrating term by term, we get

\begin{split}Z_{\rm mod}(T,\widetilde{S}^{\alpha},\lambda)=&Z_{{\rm mod},0}(T,\lambda)\sum_{k_{\alpha}\geq 0}Z_{k_{\alpha}}(T,\lambda)e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}\left(k_{\alpha}-\frac{1}{2}c_{\alpha}\right)\widetilde{S}^{\alpha}}\\
&\times\,\exp\left(\lambda^{2}\frac{E_{2}(NT)}{24}k_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}\left(k_{\beta}-\frac{1}{2}c_{\beta}\right)\right)\,,\end{split}

(4.96)

where

\begin{split}Z_{{\rm mod},0}(T,\lambda)=&\lambda^{\frac{\chi_{X}}{24}-1}\exp\left(\frac{(2\pi\mathrm{i})^{3}\widehat{\kappa}T^{3}}{6\lambda^{2}}-\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{12}\widehat{c}_{2}T+\frac{12-\chi_{B}}{96}\lambda^{2}E_{2}(NT)\right)\\
&\times\,\exp\left(\sum_{g\geq 0}f_{0}^{(g)}(T)\lambda^{2g-2}\right)\,.\end{split}

(4.97)

Note that we have once more used that
b_{2}(B)=\chi_{B}-2
,
\chi_{B}=12-a^{\alpha}a_{\alpha}
and the assumption that
a^{\alpha}=c^{\alpha}
.
Eq. (
4.90
) then implies that

Z_{H}(T,\lambda)
transforms as a Jacobi form of weight 0, index
\frac{1}{2}H\cap(H-c_{1}(B))
under
T\mapsto T/(1+NT)
.
As explained below (
2.51
), the quasiperiodicity
property (
2.50
) then follows from the manifest periodicity under
\check{\lambda}\mapsto\check{\lambda}+1
.

5
Modularity of DT invariants from monodromy

In this section, we discuss the implications of invariance under the relative conifold monodromy on various types of Donaldson-Thomas invariants. As mentioned in §
2.3
, DT invariants are invariant under auto-equivalences of the derived category
g\in\Aut\mathcal{C}
, provided
g
acts both on the charge
\gamma
and on the stability condition
\sigma
, see (
2.15
).
In general however,
g\cdot\sigma
and
\sigma
may not lie in the same chamber, so the invariants

\Omega_{\sigma}(\gamma)
and
\Omega_{\sigma}(\gamma\cdot g)
need not be equal. Here we shall assume that no wall-crossing takes place between
\sigma
and
g\cdot\sigma
, and see what kind of property this implies for the generating series of DT invariants.

The auto-equivalence of interest is the Fourier-Mukai transformation
g_{U}
with respect to the ideal sheaf of the relative diagonal discussed in §
4.2
.
It acts on the charge row vector
\gamma=(p^{0},p^{e},p^{\alpha};q_{e},q_{\alpha},q_{0})
as

\gamma\mapsto\gamma\cdot{\rm U}
with

{\rm U}=\Sigma U^{\prime-1}\Sigma^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&\frac{1}{2}c^{\beta}&-\frac{N(12-\chi_{B})}{4}&0_{\beta}&\frac{12-\chi_{B}}{4}\\
N&1&\frac{N}{2}c^{\beta}&-\frac{c_{2}N}{12}+\frac{(\chi_{B}-8)N^{2}}{4}&-\frac{N}{2}c_{\beta}&\frac{N(12-\chi_{B})}{4}\\
0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}&-\frac{N}{2}c_{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}_{\beta}&0_{\alpha}\\
0&0&0_{\beta}&1&0_{\beta}&0\\
0_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha}&-C_{\alpha\beta}&\frac{N}{2}c^{\alpha}&\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}&-\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha}\\
0&0&0_{\beta}&-N&0_{\beta}&1\end{pmatrix}

(5.1)

where
\Sigma
is the matrix appearing in the central charge
Z=\gamma\Sigma V
,

\Sigma=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0_{\beta}&-1&0&0_{\beta}\\
0&0&0_{\beta}&0&-1&0_{\beta}\\
0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&0_{\alpha\beta}&0&0&-\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}\\
0&1&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\\
0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}&0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}&0^{\alpha}_{\beta}\\
1&0&0_{\beta}&0&0&0_{\beta}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(5.2)

In view of the action (
4.37
) on Kähler moduli, the large volume (LV) limit is mapped to a phase
(T\to\frac{1}{N},S^{\alpha}\to\mathrm{i}\infty)
where the base
B
is still very large but some fibral curves have vanishing volume and fractional
B
-field
1/N
.
25
25

25

Note that the limit
(T\to 0,S^{\alpha}\to\mathrm{i}\infty)
is instead expected to correspond to the large volume limit of the relative Jacobian fibration that, for
N>1
, carries a flat but topologically non-trivial B-field.

5.1
Elliptic property of PT invariants

First, we briefly discuss how the invariance of PT invariants under the relative conifold monodromy
implies the quasi-periodicity property (
2.50
), i.e. the ‘elliptic’ part of the Jacobi properties.
Recall from §
2.2
that the PT invariant
\operatorname{PT}(k_{e},k_{\alpha},m)
counts stable
BPS states with charge

\gamma=(-1,0,0^{\alpha};-k_{e}+\frac{c_{2}}{24},-k_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha},-m)
for large volume and large B-field of suitable sign. Its image under
g_{U}
is

\gamma\cdot{\rm U}=\left(-1,0,C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}-c^{\alpha};-k_{e}+Nm-\tfrac{N(12-\chi_{B}-k_{\alpha}c^{\alpha})}{2},-m-\tfrac{12-\chi_{B}-k_{\alpha}c^{\alpha}}{2}\right)\,.

(5.3)

Further acting by a large volume monodromy (
2.2
) with
(\epsilon^{e},\epsilon^{\alpha})=(0,C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}-c^{\alpha})
, so as to set the D4-brane charge to zero, we get a PT charge vector
\gamma^{\prime}=(-1,0,0^{\alpha};-k^{\prime}_{e}+\frac{c_{2}}{24},-k^{\prime}_{\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}c^{\alpha},-m^{\prime})
with the
same horizontal D2-brane charge
k^{\prime}_{\alpha}=k_{\alpha}
but with new vertical
D2 and D0 brane charges

\quad k^{\prime}_{e}=k_{e}+\frac{N}{2}k_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}(k_{\beta}-c_{\beta})-mN,\quad m^{\prime}=m-k_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}(k_{\beta}-c_{\beta})\,.

(5.4)

For
N=1
, this reduces to the result in
[
31
, Lemma 9]
for the action of the auto-equivalence
\Phi_{H}
on the Chern character. As explained in
loc.cit.
, this autoequivalence maps stable pairs to so-called
\pi
-stable pairs, but wall-crossing
is trivial between these two notions of stability. Assuming that this statement continues to hold for genus one fibrations, we conclude that

\operatorname{PT}(k_{e},H,m)=\operatorname{PT}\left(k_{e}+N(h_{H}-1)-mN,H,m-2(h_{H}-1)\right)\,,

(5.5)

where
h_{H}=1+\frac{1}{2}k_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}(k_{\beta}-c_{\beta})
is the arithmetic genus of the curve class
H=k_{\alpha}\check{D}^{\alpha}
on the base. As explained in
[
31
, Corollary 1]
, this implies the quasi-periodicity property (
2.50
) of the generating series of PT invariants
Z_{\beta}
. It is worth stressing that (
5.5
) is only supposed to be valid for any non-trivial nef divisor class
H
on
B
. In particular it does not apply for
H=0
, due to modular anomalies in the base degree zero sector.

5.2
Modularity of D4-D2-D0 invariants

We now turn to the action of the relative conifold monodromy on D2-D0 brane BPS bound states, with charge

\gamma=(0,0,0^{\alpha};k_{e},k_{\alpha},m)
, where
k_{e}\geq 0
and
H=k_{\alpha}\check{D}^{\alpha}
is an ample divisor on the base
B
. As recalled in §
2.2
, these bound states are counted at large volume
by the genus zero GV invariant
{\rm GV}^{(0)}_{k_{e},k_{\alpha}}
, independent of the D0-brane charge
m
. Under the action of
g_{U}
, the charge vector becomes

\gamma\cdot{\rm U}=\left(0,0,-C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\alpha};-Nm+k_{e}+\frac{N}{2}c^{\alpha}k_{\alpha},k_{\alpha},m-\frac{1}{2}k_{\alpha}c^{\alpha}\right)\,.

(5.6)

After applying an homological shift
\mathcal{E}\mapsto\mathcal{E}[1]

such that
\gamma\mapsto-\gamma
, we
arrive at a D4-D2-D0 bound state with divisor class
p^{\alpha}D_{\alpha}
,
D2-brane flux
(q_{e},q_{m})
and D0-brane charge
q_{0}
given by

p^{\alpha}=C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\alpha},\ q_{e}=Nm-k_{e}-\frac{N}{2}c^{\alpha}k_{\alpha},\ q_{\alpha}=-k_{\alpha},\ q_{0}=\frac{1}{2}k_{\alpha}c^{\alpha}-m\,.

(5.7)

In particular, the D4-brane charge has no component along the section
D_{e}
(
p^{e}=0
).
In order to determine the invariants
(\mu,\hat{q}_{0})
introduced in §
2.3
,
we evaluate the quadratic form
\kappa_{ab}:=\kappa_{abc}p^{c}
:

\kappa_{ab}=\begin{pmatrix}\ell_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}&Nk_{\beta}\\
Nk_{\alpha}&0_{\alpha\beta}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(5.8)

Note that this matrix has rank 2, signature
(1,1)
, and that the vector
p^{\alpha}
is
isotropic,
p^{a}\kappa_{ab}p^{b}=\kappa_{abc}p^{a}p^{p}p^{c}=0
. This is a consequence of the divisor class
D=p^{\alpha}D_{\alpha}
being not ample.
However,
D
is nef and we therefore expect the modular properties to be as reviewed in §
2.3
, assuming that there is no wall-crossing between the chamber
g_{U}\cdot LV
and the large volume attractor chamber.

If
b_{2}(B)>1
, one needs to follow the prescription summarized below (
2.27
) and restrict
\kappa_{ab}
to the orthogonal complement of the null space spanned by
(0,\lambda_{s}^{\alpha})
where
\lambda_{s}^{\alpha},s=1,\dots,b_{2}(B)-1
is a basis of vectors such that
\lambda_{s}^{\alpha}k_{\alpha}=0
, and take its inverse.
In any case, the result is

\hat{q}_{0}=-\frac{k_{e}}{N}+\frac{\ell_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}}{2N^{2}}\,.

(5.9)

In particular, it is independent of the original D0-brane charge
m
. Indeed, the action of a large volume monodromy (
2.2
) with
\epsilon^{e}=0
shifts
m\mapsto m-k_{\alpha}\epsilon^{\alpha}
without affecting the other charges. Moreover, (
5.9
) satisfies the
Bogomolov-Gieseker bound
\hat{q}_{0}\leq\frac{\chi(\mathcal{D}_{p})}{24}=\frac{c_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}}{2}
for any
k_{e}\geq 0
, so long as the condition

(\ell_{\alpha}-N^{2}c_{\alpha})C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\alpha}\leq 0

(5.10)

is obeyed.
This inequality implies that
\eta(NT)^{12c_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}f_{k_{\alpha}}^{(0)}(T)
is regular at
T\rightarrow\mathrm{i}\infty
.
It is satisfied in all of our examples but we are not aware of a proof.

The discrete fluxes
(\mu_{e},\mu_{\alpha})
are in turn given by

\mu_{e}=Nm-k_{e}-\frac{N}{2}k_{\alpha}(k^{\alpha}+c^{\alpha})-\epsilon^{e}k^{\alpha}\ell_{\alpha}-N\epsilon^{\alpha}k_{\alpha}\ ,\quad\mu_{\alpha}=-k_{\alpha}-N\epsilon^{e}k_{\alpha}

(5.11)

where
\epsilon^{e},\epsilon^{\alpha}
are arbitrary integers. Modulo these spectral flow parameters,
and restricting to the space
\lambda_{s}^{\alpha}\mu_{\alpha}=0
, the fluxes
(\mu_{e},\mu_{\alpha})

are a priori valued in the discriminant group
\mathds{Z}_{kN}\times\mathds{Z}_{kN}
, of cardinality
(kN)^{2}
, where

k
is the greatest common divisor of the
k_{\alpha}
’s.
Note that
k_{\alpha}(k^{\alpha}+c^{\alpha})=2k_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}-k_{\alpha}(k^{\alpha}-c^{\alpha})
.
Since
k_{\alpha}(k^{\alpha}-c^{\alpha})
is the Euler characteristic of a curve in the class
k_{\alpha}
, which is even, we conclude that
\frac{N}{2}k_{\alpha}(k^{\alpha}+c^{\alpha})
is an integer multiple of
N
, as long as the class can be represented by a smooth curve in
B
.

The assumption that there is no wall-crossing between
g_{U}\cdot LV
and the large volume attractor chamber implies the equality of
integer DT invariants

\Omega_{\infty}(0,0,0;k_{e},k_{\alpha},m)={\rm GV}^{(0)}_{k_{e},k_{\alpha}}=\Omega_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu_{e},\mu_{\alpha}}(\hat{q}_{0})\,.

(5.12)

In particular, the fact that the D2-D0 brane index is independent of the D0-brane charge (or holomorphic Euler characteristic)
m
implies that the corresponding D4-D2-D0 indices on the r.h.s. of (
5.12
) are invariant under
\mu_{e}\mapsto\mu_{e}+N
, rather than just
\mu_{e}\mapsto\mu_{e}+kN
. While the origin of this invariance is obscure on the D4-D2-D0 side, its effect is to cut down the number of independent fluxes to

kN^{2}
, further halved by the symmetry
\mu\mapsto-\mu
. For a fixed value of
\mu_{\alpha}
, there are effectively
N
components, and we shall later argue that only the coset
\mu_{\alpha}=-k_{\alpha}
is populated.

Before discussing the implications of the relation (
5.12
) at the level of generating series of GW and DT invariants, we remark that for D2-D0 branes wrapping the genus-one fiber (i.e. for
\gamma=(0,0,0^{\alpha};k_{e},0,m)
), the image under the relative conifold monodromy
g_{U}
has vanishing D4-brane charge,

\gamma\cdot{\rm U}=(0,0,0;k_{e}-Nm,0,m)

(5.13)

and the corresponding BPS states are counted at large volume by the genus 0 GV invariant

{\rm GV}_{k_{e}-Nm,0}^{(0)}
. Assuming again the absence of wall-crossing, and using the
m
-independence of the DT invariant counting the original BPS states of charge
\gamma
, we conclude that
{\rm GV}_{k_{e},0}^{(0)}
should be invariant under
k_{e}\mapsto k_{e}+N
. This gives a heuristic derivation of the periodicity property stated above (
2.38
), although it does not say anything about higher genus base degree zero GV invariants.

5.2.1
Relating generating series of GW and rank 0 DT invariants

Now, we explore the relation between the generating series
f_{k^{\alpha}}(T)

of genus 0 GW invariants and the generating series
h_{p;\mu}(\tau)
of D4-D2-D0 invariants,
which we recall for convenience,

f_{k_{\alpha}}(T)=\sum_{k_{e}\geq 0}{\rm GW}_{k_{e},k_{\alpha}}^{(0)}q_{T}^{k_{e}-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}{2N}}\,,\quad h_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu_{e},\mu_{\alpha}}(\tau)=\sum_{\hat{q}_{0}\leq\frac{\chi(\mathcal{D}_{p})}{24}}\bar{\Omega}_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu_{e},\mu_{\alpha}}(\hat{q}_{0})\,q_{\tau}^{-\hat{q}_{0}}\,.

(5.14)

Note that we again drop the genus superscript of
f_{k_{\alpha}}^{(0)}(T)
.
We further introduce the generating series of
integer
D4-D2-D0 invariants,

h^{\rm int}_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu_{e},\mu_{\alpha}}(\tau):=\sum_{\hat{q}_{0}\leq\frac{\chi(\mathcal{D}_{p})}{24}}\Omega_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu_{e},\mu_{\alpha}}(\hat{q}_{0})\,q_{\tau}^{-\hat{q}_{0}}\,.

(5.15)

We will first show that

\displaystyle f_{k_{\alpha}}^{\rm GV}(T)=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\mu_{e}=0}^{N-1}h_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu_{e},k_{\alpha}}^{\rm int}\left(NT\right)\,.

(5.16)

To this end, note that for
p^{0}=p^{e}=0
,
p^{\alpha}=k^{\alpha}
and
\mu_{\alpha}=-k_{\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{q}_{0}=q_{0}+\frac{1}{2}k_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}-\left(\frac{\mu_{e}}{N}-\frac{k_{\alpha}\ell^{\alpha}}{2N^{2}}\right)\,,

(5.17)

and since
q_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}-\frac{1}{2}c_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}
, and
k^{\alpha}(k_{\alpha}-c_{\alpha})\in 2\mathbb{Z}
, we can write

\displaystyle h^{\rm int}_{0,k^{\alpha};-\mu_{e},-k_{\alpha}}(\tau)=\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\in\mathbb{Z}}}{{n\geq n_{\rm m}}}}\Omega_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu_{e},k_{\alpha}}\left(-n-\frac{\mu_{e}}{N}+\frac{k_{\alpha}\ell^{\alpha}}{2N^{2}}\right)\,q_{\tau}^{n+\frac{\mu_{e}}{N}-\frac{k_{\alpha}\ell^{\alpha}}{2N^{2}}}\,,

(5.18)

where
n_{\rm m}:=\mu_{e}/N-k_{\alpha}(c^{\alpha}-\ell^{\alpha}/N^{2})/2
.
Using (
5.12
) we then observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{k_{e}\geq 0}{\rm GV}_{k_{e},k_{\alpha}}^{(0)}q_{T}^{k_{e}-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}{2N}}=\sum_{k_{e}\geq 0}\Omega_{0,k^{\alpha};-k_{e},-k_{\alpha}}\left(-\tfrac{k_{e}}{N}+\tfrac{\ell_{\alpha}}{2N^{2}}k^{\alpha}\right)q_{T}^{k_{e}-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}{2N}}\\
=&\sum\limits_{\mu_{e}=0}^{N-1}\sum\limits_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\Omega_{0,k^{\alpha};-\mu_{e},-k_{\alpha}}\left(-n-\tfrac{\mu_{e}}{N}+\tfrac{\ell_{\alpha}}{2N^{2}}k^{\alpha}\right)q_{T}^{Nn+\mu_{e}-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}{2N}}\,.\end{split}

(5.19)

The result (
5.16
) follows after taking into account the symmetry under
\mu\rightarrow-\mu
.

In order to obtain the relation between genus 0 GW invariants and rational DT invariants,
we use the multicover formulae, which crucially involve different powers of
d
in the sum over divisors:

{\rm GW}^{(0)}_{k_{e},k_{\alpha}}=\sum_{d|(k_{e},k_{\alpha})}\frac{1}{d^{3}}{\rm GV}^{(0)}_{k_{e}/d,k_{\alpha}/d}\ ,\quad\bar{\Omega}_{t}(\gamma)=\sum_{d|\gamma}\frac{1}{d^{2}}\Omega_{t}(\gamma/d)

(5.20)

or equivalently

{\rm GV}^{(0)}_{k_{e},k_{\alpha}}=\sum_{d|(k_{e},k_{\alpha})}\frac{\mu(d)}{d^{3}}{\rm GW}^{(0)}_{k_{e}/d,k_{\alpha}/d}\ ,\quad\Omega_{t}(\gamma)=\sum_{d|\gamma}\frac{\mu(d)}{d^{2}}\bar{\Omega}_{t}(\gamma/d)

(5.21)

where
\mu(d)
is the Möbius function.
Morever, it is important to stress that the statement of
m
-independence holds only for the integer DT invariants in (
5.12
), rather than for their rational counterparts. Nonetheless, we claim that
the relation between generating series of GW and rational DT invariants is almost identical to (
5.16
), namely

k\,f_{k_{\alpha}}(T)=\sum_{\mu_{e}=0}^{kN-1}h_{0,k^{\alpha},\mu_{e},k_{\alpha}}(NT)\,,

(5.22)

where we recall that
k
is the greatest common divisor of the
k_{\alpha}
’s.

To show this, we first observe that for a function

\displaystyle f(T)=\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}a_{n}e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}T(n+\phi)}\,,\quad a_{n}\in\mathbb{C}\,,\,\,\phi\in\mathbb{Q}\,,

(5.23)

and any
N\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
,
k\in\{0,\ldots,N-1\}
, the restricted sum over
n=k\mod N
is given by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum\limits_{b=0}^{N-1}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu}{N}(k+\phi)}f\left(T+\frac{b}{N}\right)=\sum\limits_{n\geq 0}a_{Nn+k}e^{2\pi\mathrm{i}T(Nn+k+\phi)}\,.

(5.24)

We can therefore invert the relation
(
5.16
) into

h^{\rm int}_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu,k_{\alpha}}(NT)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{b=0}^{N-1}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}b}{N}(\mu-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}{2N})}f_{k_{\alpha}}^{{\rm GV}}\left(T+\tfrac{b}{N}\right)

(5.25)

and insert it into the definition of
h_{p;\mu}
,

\displaystyle h_{0,k^{\alpha};\mu,k_{\alpha}}(NT)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{d|(\mu,k)}\frac{1}{d^{2}}h^{\rm int}_{0,k^{\alpha}/d;\mu/d,k_{\alpha}/d}(dNT)

(5.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{d|(\mu,k^{\alpha})}\frac{1}{Nd^{2}}\sum_{b=0}^{N-1}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}b}{dN}(\mu-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}{2N})}\sum_{e|(k/d)}\frac{\mu(e)}{e^{3}}f_{k_{\alpha}/(de)}\left(deT+\tfrac{b}{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m|k}\frac{1}{m^{3}}\left[\sum_{d|(\mu,m)}\frac{d}{N}\mu(m/d)\sum_{b=0}^{N-1}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}b}{dN}(\mu-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}{2N})}\right]f_{k_{\alpha}/m}\left(mT+\tfrac{b}{N}\right)\,.

Summing over
\mu_{e}
and using the identities

\forall d,\sum_{\mu=0\dots kN-1\atop d|\mu}e^{-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}b\mu}{dN}}=\frac{kN}{d}\delta^{(N)}_{b}\ ;\quad\forall m,\sum_{d|m}\mu(m/d)=\delta_{m,1}

(5.27)

we arrive exactly at (
5.22
).

5.2.2
Comparing holomorphic anomaly equations

Let us now compare the holomorphic anomaly equations satisfied by generating series of GW and rank 0 DT invariants. For simplicity we continue to restrict to genus one fibrations over
\mathds{P}^{2}
, such that the index
\alpha
takes only one value, which we omit.

We assume that the divisor class
p^{a}=(0,k)
lies on the boundary of the effective cone, such that the only possible decompositions
p=\sum_{i=1}^{n}p_{i}
are of the form
p_{i}=(0,k_{i})
. As a result, only two-term decompositions contribute,
k=k_{1}+k_{2}
. We denote
k_{0}=\gcd(k_{1},k_{2})
.
According to the prescription (
2.28
), the holomorphic anomaly for rank 0 DT invariants is given by
26
26

26

For fibrations on
\mathds{P}^{2}
, the lattice
\Lambda_{\perp}
is trivial
and one does not need any non-trivial glue vectors, so
n_{g}=1,g_{A}=0
.

\begin{split}\partial_{\bar{\tau}}\widehat{h}_{0,k;\mu_{e},\mu}=&\frac{1}{16\pi\mathrm{i}\tau_{2}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}k_{0}\sum_{\mu_{1}\in\mathds{Z}_{k_{1}N}\atop\mu_{2}\in\mathds{Z}_{k_{2}N}}\delta^{(k_{0}N)}_{\mu-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}}\,\delta^{(Nkk_{1}k_{2}/k_{0}^{2})}_{\mu_{12}}\\
&\times\sum_{\mu_{1,e}\in\mathds{Z}_{k_{1}N}\atop\mu_{2,e}\in\mathds{Z}_{k_{2}N}}\delta^{(k_{0}N)}_{\mu_{e}-\mu_{1,e}-\mu_{2,e}-\frac{\ell}{N}(\mu-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})}\,\widehat{h}_{0,k_{1};\mu_{1,e},\mu_{1}}\widehat{h}_{0,k_{2};\mu_{2,e},\mu_{2}}\end{split}

(5.28)

where

k_{0}\mu_{12}=k_{2}\mu_{1}-k_{1}\mu_{2}+Nk_{1}k_{2}(\rho_{1}^{e}-\rho_{2}^{e})

(5.29)

with
(\rho^{e}_{1},\rho^{e}_{2})
any integral solutions to

\mu-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}=N(k_{1}\rho_{1}^{e}+k_{2}\rho_{2}^{e})\,.

(5.30)

Let us focus on the case
\mu=k
.
Note that for
k=k_{1}+k_{2}
one has
\gcd(k,k_{1})=\gcd(k_{1},k_{2})=k_{0}
and introduce
\hat{k}:=k/k_{0}
,
\hat{k}_{1}:=k_{1}/k_{0}
,
\hat{k}_{2}:=k_{2}/k_{0}
.
Then we can solve (
5.30
) for
\rho_{2}^{e}
and find that the second
\delta
-function in (
5.28
) imposes

\displaystyle\hat{k}\mu_{1}-\hat{k}k_{1}+Nk_{1}\hat{k}\rho_{1}^{e}=0\text{ mod }Nk_{1}\hat{k}_{2}\hat{k}\,.

(5.31)

This implies that
\hat{k}\mu_{1}-\hat{k}k_{1}=0\text{ mod }Nk_{1}\hat{k}
.
Solving (
5.30
) instead for
\rho_{1}^{e}
, one also obtains that
\hat{k}\mu_{2}-\hat{k}k_{2}=0\text{ mod }Nk_{2}\hat{k}
.
We therefore find that the sum only receives contributions from terms with

\displaystyle\mu_{1}=k_{1}\text{ mod }Nk_{1}\,,\quad\mu_{2}=k_{2}\text{ mod }Nk_{2}\,.

(5.32)

All of these terms are compatible with
\rho_{1}^{e}=\rho_{2}^{e}=0
and the holomorphic anomaly equations take the simplified form

\displaystyle\partial_{\bar{\tau}}\widehat{h}_{0,k;\mu_{e},k}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi\mathrm{i}\tau_{2}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}k_{0}\sum_{\mu_{1,e}\in\mathds{Z}_{k_{1}N}\atop\mu_{2,e}\in\mathds{Z}_{k_{2}N}}\delta^{(k_{0}N)}_{\mu_{e}-\mu_{1,e}-\mu_{2,e}}\,\widehat{h}_{0,k_{1};\mu_{1,e},k_{1}}\widehat{h}_{0,k_{2};\mu_{2,e},k_{2}}\,.

(5.33)

In terms of the sum over all residue classes
\overline{h_{0,k}}:=\sum_{\mu_{e}\in\mathds{Z}_{kN}}\widehat{h}_{0,k;\mu_{e},k}
, this further simplifies to

\partial_{\bar{\tau}}\overline{h_{0,k}}=\frac{k}{16\pi\mathrm{i}\tau_{2}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\overline{h_{0,k_{1}}}\,\overline{h_{0,k_{2}}}\,.

(5.34)

Identifying
\overline{h_{0,k}}(N\tau)=k\widehat{f}_{k}(T)
, we arrive at

\partial_{\bar{T}}\widehat{f}_{k}(T)=\frac{1}{16\pi\mathrm{i}NT_{2}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}k_{1}k_{2}\ \widehat{f}_{k_{1}}\widehat{f}_{k_{2}}

(5.35)

or equivalently, using
\widehat{E}_{2}(NT)=E_{2}(NT)-\frac{3}{\pi NT_{2}}
,
\partial_{\bar{T}}\widehat{E}_{2}(NT)=\frac{3\mathrm{i}}{2\pi NT_{2}^{2}}
,

\partial_{\widehat{E}_{2}(NT)}\widehat{f}_{k}=-\frac{1}{24}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}k_{1}k_{2}\ \widehat{f}_{k_{1}}\widehat{f}_{k_{2}}\,.

(5.36)

This reproduces the holomorphic anomaly equation (
4.64
) for genus 0 GW invariants.
Generalizing this argument to the case of genus-one fibrations on higher del Pezzo surfaces would require to confront both the isotropicity of
p^{a}
and non-invertibility of
\kappa_{ab}
simultaneously, which we leave for future work.

5.2.3
Vector valued modularity

So far, we have been able to express the genus zero free energies in modular polarization as sums over modular completions of D4-D2-D0 indices

\displaystyle k\widehat{f}_{k}(T)=\sum_{\mu_{e}=0}^{kN-1}\widehat{h}_{0,k,\mu_{e},k}\left(NT\right)\,.

(5.37)

However, this relation only involves contributions where the coset of the base curve class
\mu\in\Lambda^{*}/\Lambda
is equal to the image
k
of the D4-brane charge in
\Lambda^{*}
.
This is closely related to the fact that
\widehat{f}_{k}(T)
is a modular form for
\Gamma_{1}(N)
but transforms itself as a vector valued modular form under the full modular group
[
34
,
35
,
40
]
.

In
[
35
]
it was proposed that – for
N>1
– the image of
f_{k}(T)
under the Fricke involution
T\rightarrow-1/(NT)
can be interpreted as a genus zero topological string free energy
f^{\vee}_{k}(T)
of the relative Jacobian fibration
J
of
X
, in the presence of a flat but topologically non-trivial B-field.
Using (
2.24
), we find that under S-transformations

\displaystyle\begin{split}&k\,\widehat{f}_{k}\left(-\frac{1}{NT}\right)=\sum_{\mu_{e}=0}^{kN-1}\widehat{h}_{0,k,\mu_{e},k}\left(-\frac{1}{T}\right)\\
=&\frac{e^{\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{4}\left[c_{\alpha}\left(4k^{\alpha}-c^{\alpha}\right)+1\right]}}{kN}\sum_{\mu_{e}^{\prime}=0}^{kN-1}\sum_{k^{\prime}=0}^{kN-1}\sum_{\mu_{e}=0}^{kN-1}\exp\left(-\tfrac{2\pi\mathrm{i}}{N}\left[\tfrac{k^{\prime}}{k}\left(\mu_{e}-\tfrac{k\ell}{N}\right)+\mu_{e}^{\prime}\right]\right)\widehat{h}_{0,k,\mu_{e}^{\prime},k^{\prime}}\left(T\right)\\
=&(-1)^{c_{\alpha}k^{\alpha}}e^{\frac{\pi\mathrm{i}}{4}(\chi_{B}-3)}\sum_{\mu_{e}^{\prime}=0}^{kN-1}\exp\left(-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{e}^{\prime}}{N}\right)\widehat{h}_{0,k,\mu_{e}^{\prime},0}\left(T\right)\,.\end{split}

(5.38)

This suggests that
f^{\vee}_{k}(T)
also admits a modular completion
\widehat{f}_{k}^{\vee}(T)
, that satisfies

\displaystyle k\widehat{f}^{\vee}_{k}(T)=\sum_{\mu_{e}=0}^{kN-1}\exp\left(-\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}\mu_{e}}{N}\right)\widehat{h}_{0,k,\mu_{e},0}\left(T\right)\,.

(5.39)

Perhaps the most striking aspect of this relation is that the two sides a priori count objects on different geometries.

More generally, the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
orbit of
\widehat{f}_{k}(T)
is composed of topological string free energies associated to different genus one fibrations that share the same relative Jacobian fibration with
X
and carry different B-field topologies
[
35
,
40
]
.
By applying suitable combinations of the
T
- and
S
-transformations (
2.24
), it is possible to obtain relations analogous to (
5.37
) and (
5.38
) for all of those string backgrounds.
However, while these relations will be interesting to explore further, they would take us too far afield and we will leave this for future work.

5.3
Comments on the black string SCFT

We now comment on the growth of D4-D2-D0 indices, and its relation to the central charge of the black string SCFT obtained by wrapping an M5-brane on the corresponding divisor
[
94
]
. First, let us consider the generating series of genus 0 GW invariants (which have the same growth as genus 0 GV invariants)

f_{H}(T)=-\frac{\Delta_{2N}(T)^{r_{H}/N}\varphi_{H}(T,0)}{\eta(NT)^{12c_{1}(B)\cdot H}}\,.

(5.40)

Since
f_{H}(T):=\sum_{n}C_{H}(n)q^{n}
is a
\Gamma_{1}(N)
weak quasimodular form of negative weight, the growth of its Fourier coefficients is controlled by the most polar term
q^{\prime-\Delta_{\rm max}}
in the Fourier expansion around the cusp at
T=0
(where
q^{\prime}:=e^{-2\pi\mathrm{i}/T}
),

C_{H}(n)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\to+\infty}}{{\sim}}\exp\left(4\pi\sqrt{\Delta_{\rm max}n}\right)\,.

(5.41)

Using the fact that
\Delta_{2N}(T)
is regular near
T=0

27
27

27

Following
[
35
, (4.21)]
, one finds that
T^{2N}\Delta_{2N}\left(-\frac{1}{NT}\right)=T^{2N}\exp\left(\frac{2\pi\mathrm{i}}{NT}\right)\phi_{-2,1}\left(-\frac{1}{T},-\frac{1}{NT}\right)^{-N}=\phi_{-2,1}\left(T,-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{-N}=\left[2\mathrm{i}\sin(\pi/N)\right]^{-2N}+\mathcal{O}(q^{\prime})
.
, while
\eta(NT)
behaves as
q^{\prime\frac{1}{24N}}
, we find

\Delta_{\rm max}=\frac{c_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}}{2N}\,.

(5.42)

This should be contrasted with the pole at
T=\mathrm{i}\infty
, which has order
\ell_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}/(2N)
. Going back to (
5.7
) and identifying the Fourier mode
n
with
k_{e}-\frac{\ell_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}}{2N}=-N\hat{q}_{0}
, we find that the D4-D2-D0 indices with
p=(0,C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta})
grow as

\Omega_{p,\mu}(\hat{q}_{0})\sim\exp\left(2\pi\sqrt{2c_{\alpha}C^{\alpha\beta}k_{\beta}(-\hat{q}_{0})}\right)\,.

(5.43)

This agrees with the macroscopic Bekenstein-Hawking entropy

S_{BH}=2\pi\sqrt{\frac{p^{3}+c_{2}\cdot p}{6}(-\hat{q}_{0})}

(5.44)

upon noting that
p^{3}:=\kappa_{abc}p^{a}p^{b}p^{c}=0
and
c_{\alpha}=\frac{1}{12}c_{2,\alpha}
. As explained in
[
94
]
, this growth is consistent with the central charge of the
(0,4)
superconformal field theory obtained by wrapping an M5-brane on a very ample divisor
28
28

28

The prime is used to distinguish this result, based on the ampleness assumption, from the correct result in (
5.51
) below.

\begin{split}c^{\prime}_{L}=&\chi(D)=\kappa_{abc}p^{a}p^{b}p^{c}+c_{2,a}p^{a}=12c_{1}(B)\cdot C\,,\\
c^{\prime}_{R}=&6\chi(\mathcal{O}(D))=\kappa_{abc}p^{a}p^{b}p^{c}+\frac{1}{2}c_{2a}p^{a}=6c_{1}(B)\cdot C\,.\end{split}

(5.45)

However, as emphasized in
[
95
,
96
]
, the divisor
D=\pi^{-1}(C)
is not ample and the formulae (
5.45
) for the central charges do not apply.
In particular, the odd cohomology of
D

does not in general vanish, rather it is given by the arithmetic genus of the curve
C
,

h_{1,0}(D)=h_{D}:=\frac{1}{2}(C^{2}-c_{1}(B)\cdot C)+1\,.

(5.46)

Using the Euler number
\chi(D)
and the signature
\sigma(D)
given by

\displaystyle\chi(D)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2-4h_{1,0}(D)+2h_{2,0}(D)+h_{1,1}(D)=12c_{1}(B)\cdot C\,,

\displaystyle\sigma(D)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2+2h_{2,0}(D)-h_{1,1}(D)=-8c_{1}(B)\cdot C\,,

(5.47)

\displaystyle\chi(\mathcal{O}_{D})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-h_{1,0}(D)+h_{2,0}(D)=c_{1}(B)\cdot C\,,

we deduce the even cohomology of
D
,

\begin{split}h_{2,0}(D)=&\frac{1}{2}\left(C^{2}+c_{1}(B)\cdot C\right)\,,\\
h_{1,1}(D)=&C^{2}+9c_{1}(B)\cdot C+2\,.\end{split}

(5.48)

Recall that the Hodge numbers
h_{2,0},h_{1,1}
are related to the number of self-dual and anti-self-dual two-forms by

b_{2}^{+}(D)=2h_{2,0}(D)+1\,,\quad b_{2}^{-}(D)=h_{1,1}(D)-1\,,

(5.49)

while the holomorphic Euler number
\chi(\mathcal{O}_{D})=\frac{1}{4}(\chi(D)+\sigma(D))
determines the cohomology of the line bundle
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)
,

\chi(\mathcal{O}_{D})=\sum_{i=0}^{3}(-1)^{i}\dim H^{i}(X,\mathcal{L})\,.

(5.50)

For a very ample line bundle,
h_{1,0}(D)
vanishes and
\chi(\mathcal{O}_{D})=\dim H^{0}(X,\mathcal{L})
gives the complex dimension (plus one) of the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{D}
of the divisor
D
inside
X
. More generally however, the real dimension of
\mathcal{M}_{D}
is given by
d_{D}=2h_{2,0}(D)
.

The
(0,4)
SCFT obtained by wrapping an M5-brane on
D
(or a D3-brane on
C
in F-theory) is, at least at large volume, a non-linear sigma model whose target space is a Narain bundle of signature
(b_{2}^{-}-1,b_{2}^{+}-1)
over
\mathcal{M}_{D}
, along with a center of motion factor
\mathds{R}^{3}\times S^{1}
. The central charges (including the center of motion factor) are given by
[
95
,
96
]

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{L}=&d_{D}+b_{2}^{-}+\frac{1}{2}\times 4h_{1,0}(D)+3=3C^{2}+9c_{1}(B)\cdot C+6\,,\\
c_{R}=&d_{D}+b_{2}^{+}+\frac{1}{2}\times 4h_{2,0}(D)+5=3C^{2}+3c_{1}(B)\cdot C+6\,.\end{split}

(5.51)

In terms of the arithmetic genus (
5.46
), this is rewritten as

c_{L}=6h_{D}+12c_{1}(B)\cdot C\,,\quad c_{R}=6h_{D}+6c_{1}(B)\cdot C\,,

(5.52)

which differs from (
5.45
) by an additional term
6h_{D}
on both sides (recall that the
difference
c_{L}-c_{R}
is fixed by the gravitational anomaly
[
97
]
).

This presents a puzzle, since the growth of the D4-D2-D0 indices is controlled by
c^{\prime}_{L}
, in agreement with the macroscopic entropy, rather than by
c_{L}
. The most obvious resolution is that even though the
central charge of the SCFT is given by (
5.51
), the Cardy formula involves the

effective
central charge
c_{\rm eff}=c_{L}-24h_{\rm min}
, where
h_{\rm min}
is the conformal dimension of the lowest conformal primary, which reduces to
c^{\prime}_{L}
if one
identifies
h_{\rm min}=\frac{1}{4}h_{D}
, which is recognized as the energy of the Ramond ground state for
4h_{D}
real fermions. We note that the difference
(c_{L}-c^{\prime}_{L},c_{R}-c^{\prime}_{R})
can also be understood as the contribution of one unit of D4-brane charge
p^{e}
along the base; on the F-theory side, this corresponds to one unit of Taub-NUT charge, and is consistent with the 4D/5D lift
[
98
,
99
]
.

On the other hand, the authors of
[
96
]
presented some support for the formulae
(
5.51
) from the growth of the Fourier coefficients of the meromorphic Jacobi form
Z_{H}(T,\lambda)=\sum_{n,r}C(n,r)q^{n}y^{r}
, tentatively identified as the elliptic genus of the SCFT. In particular, in
[
96
, §A]
is is argued that for a meromorphic Jacobi form of index
k
under
SL(2,\mathds{Z})
, Fourier coefficients grow as
C(n,r)\sim\exp(\pi\sqrt{4kn-r^{2}})
(similarly as for
a weak Jacobi form of the same index). The same arguments show that for a meromorphic Jacobi form of index
k
under
\Gamma_{1}(N)
(for example one obtained from
a
SL(2,\mathds{Z})
modular form by rescaling
(T,\lambda)\to(NT,\lambda)
), Fourier coefficients grow
as
C(n,r)\sim\exp(\pi\sqrt{\frac{4kn}{N}-r^{2}})
. Setting
k=h_{D}-1
and taking the large
n
limit at fixed
r
, we get

\log|C(n,r)|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle n\to+\infty}}{{\sim}}2\pi\sqrt{\frac{(C^{2}-c_{1}(B)\cdot C+2)n}{2N}}\,.

(5.53)

Let us compare this result with the macroscopic entropy of a static BMPV black hole, given by
[
100
]

S_{5D}(q_{a})=2\pi\left(\frac{1}{6}\kappa_{abc}t^{a}t^{b}t^{c}\right)\,,\quad q_{a}=\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{abc}t^{b}t^{c}\,,

(5.54)

where
t^{a}
corresponds to the Kahler modulus at the attractor point. For genus one fibrations on
\mathds{P}^{2}
, with charge
q_{a}=(n,C)
, setting
\hat{n}=n-\frac{\ell\cdot C}{2N}

we find

S_{5D}(n,C)=\frac{2\pi\sqrt{\hat{n}N-\sqrt{N\left(\hat{n}^{2}N-\tilde{\kappa}C^{2}\right)}}}{3\sqrt{\widehat{\kappa}N^{3}}}\left(\sqrt{N\left(\hat{n}^{2}N-\tilde{\kappa}C^{2}\right)}+2\hat{n}N\right)

(5.55)

provided
|\hat{n}|\geq\sqrt{\frac{\widehat{\kappa}}{N}}|C|
.
In the Cardy regime
\tilde{n}\gg|C|
, we get

S_{5D}(n,C)=2\pi\sqrt{\frac{\hat{n}C^{2}}{2N}}-\frac{\pi\widehat{\kappa}(C^{2})^{3/2}}{3(2N\hat{n})^{3/2}}+\dots\simeq 2\pi\sqrt{\frac{\hat{n}C^{2}}{2N}}\left(1-\frac{\widehat{\kappa}C^{2}}{24N\hat{n}}+\dots\right)\,.

(5.56)

The
N
-dependence at leading order is consistent with the expectation for
\Gamma_{1}(N)
Jacobi forms, but the microscopic origin of the
\mathcal{O}(\widehat{\kappa}C^{2}/\tilde{m})
corrections is obscure. Subleading corrections proportional to
c_{1}(B)
were matched in
[
101
